I never had any reason to think dispatch was watching me or talking about me on the radio before then. It's just... this is the first time it's been happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of feeling unusually energized or euphoric, or the opposite—very depressed—since this started?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really felt anything like that. I mean, it's definitely stressful dealing with the tracking and the radio stuff... it makes me anxious. But I wouldn't say I've been super energized or really depressed. Just mostly trying to figure out why they're doing this.\n\nDoctor: Have you found your thoughts or speech becoming hard for other people to follow?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. Um, people seem to understand me just fine. Nobody at work or anywhere else has told me I'm hard to follow or that I'm not making sense. So, yeah, I haven't really noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Since these experiences started, have you had a period when your mood was unusually elevated or expansive?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. Um, I think that's kind of like what you just asked a minute ago, but yeah... I haven't had any times where I felt unusually high or, you know, elevated. It's really just been the stress of dealing with the dispatch situation.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself becoming less interested in things or spending much less time interacting with other people than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I have. I just... I don't really feel like singing with my choir anymore, and I haven't been having the softball team over like I used to. Most days, honestly, after my shift I just sit at the kitchen table. I'll have my planner open right in front of me, but I just can't seem to make myself start dinner, or make any calls, or really do anything else. I just kind of sit there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices that other people around you could not hear?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't experienced anything like that. I've never heard voices or anything that wasn't actually there. It's just the stress and feeling stuck, like I mentioned. But no voices.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there’s any chance that the dispatch and radio comments might be a misunderstanding or explainable in some other way?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think it's a misunderstanding. People tell me it's just normal oversight, but I know what I'm seeing. Dispatch and compliance, they've been tracking my route changes through the computer system. And it's the little comments on the radio... I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me. It's not just a coincidence or a mistake. I know they're doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any period during these two months when your mood was unusually depressed?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I've been depressed. I mean, I'm tired and stressed out from everything going on at work, and I just don't have the energy for things like choir or softball right now. But it's not like I'm sad or crying all the time. I just feel kind of blank and drained.\n\nDoctor: Have other people noticed that you seem emotionally flat or show much less expression than you used to?\n\nPatient: I don't know if they'd say I look flat, exactly, but... they've definitely noticed I'm not acting like myself. Like I mentioned, I just don't feel like singing with the choir or having the softball team over anymore. People have asked about that. And at home... most days after my shift, I just end up sitting at the kitchen table. I'll have my planner open, but I just can't make myself start dinner, or make any calls, or really do anything else. I just sort of sit there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a stretch of at least two weeks when you were still having the tracking or radio beliefs, but your mood was otherwise normal?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to answer that... I mean, since I started noticing the tracking and the comments, I haven't really felt normal at all. It's just been this same feeling the whole time. Just stressed and drained. \n\nThere hasn't really been a couple of weeks where I felt completely fine or like my old self while all that was going on. It's just been pretty much constant since it started.\n\nDoctor: Have these beliefs and the change in your functioning been going on continuously for at least six months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's been longer than six months. I'll have my planner open right in front of me, but I just can't seem to make myself start dinner, or make any calls, or really do anything else. I just kind of sit there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices that other people around you could not hear?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't experienced anything like that. I've never heard voices or anything that wasn't actually there. It's just the stress and feeling stuck, like I mentioned. People tell me it's just normal oversight, but I know what I'm seeing. Dispatch and compliance, they've been tracking my route changes through the computer system. And it's the little comments on the radio... I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me. It's not just a coincidence or a mistake. I know they're doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any period during these two months when your mood was unusually depressed?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I've been depressed. I just feel kind of blank and drained.\n\nDoctor: Have other people noticed that you seem emotionally flat or show much less expression than you used to?\n\nPatient: I don't know if they'd say I look flat, exactly, but... they've definitely noticed I'm not acting like myself. I never had any reason to think dispatch was watching me or talking about me on the radio before then. It's just... this is the first time it's been happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of feeling unusually energized or euphoric, or the opposite—very depressed—since this started?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really felt anything like that. I mean, it's definitely stressful dealing with the tracking and the radio stuff... it makes me anxious. But I wouldn't say I've been super energized or really depressed. Just mostly trying to figure out why they're doing this.\n\nDoctor: Have you found your thoughts or speech becoming hard for other people to follow?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. Um, people seem to understand me just fine. Nobody at work or anywhere else has told me I'm hard to follow or that I'm not making sense. So, yeah, I haven't really noticed anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Since these experiences started, have you had a period when your mood was unusually elevated or expansive?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. Um, I think that's kind of like what you just asked a minute ago, but yeah... I haven't had any times where I felt unusually high or, you know, elevated. It's really just been the stress of dealing with the dispatch situation.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself becoming less interested in things or spending much less time interacting with other people than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I have. I just... I don't really feel like singing with my choir anymore, and I haven't been having the softball team over like I used to. Most days, honestly, after my shift I just sit at the kitchen table. I'll have my planner open right in front of me, but I just can't seem to make myself start dinner, or make any calls, or really do anything else. I just kind of sit there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices that other people around you could not hear?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't experienced anything like that. I've never heard voices or anything that wasn't actually there. It's just the stress and feeling stuck, like I mentioned. But no voices.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there’s any chance that the dispatch and radio comments might be a misunderstanding or explainable in some other way?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think it's a misunderstanding. People tell me it's just normal oversight, but I know what I'm seeing. Dispatch and compliance, they've been tracking my route changes through the computer system. And it's the little comments on the radio... I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me. It's not just a coincidence or a mistake. I know they're doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any period during these two months when your mood was unusually depressed?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I've been depressed. I mean, I'm tired and stressed out from everything going on at work, and I just don't have the energy for things like choir or softball right now. But it's not like I'm sad or crying all the time. I just feel kind of blank and drained.\n\nDoctor: Have other people noticed that you seem emotionally flat or show much less expression than you used to?\n\nPatient: I don't know if they'd say I look flat, exactly, but... they've definitely noticed I'm not acting like myself. Like I mentioned, I just don't feel like singing with the choir or having the softball team over anymore. People have asked about that. And at home... most days after my shift, I just end up sitting at the kitchen table. I'll have my planner open, but I just can't make myself start dinner, or make any calls, or really do anything else. I just sort of sit there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a stretch of at least two weeks when you were still having the tracking or radio beliefs, but your mood was otherwise normal?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to answer that... I mean, since I started noticing the tracking and the comments, I haven't really felt normal at all. It's just been this same feeling the whole time. Just stressed and drained. \n\nThere hasn't really been a couple of weeks where I felt completely fine or like my old self while all that was going on. It's just been pretty much constant since it started.\n\nDoctor: Have these beliefs and the change in your functioning been going on continuously for at least six months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's been longer than six months. I'd say... this has really been building for about eight months now. It all started right around the time they put me in the temporary dispatch lead role at work.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen things that other people around you could not see?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I mean, I hear the comments, like I mentioned before, but... no, I don't see things. I've never seen anything that wasn't actually there.\n\nDoctor: During the past eight months, have you had any clear episode of major depression—meaning at least two weeks of feeling depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I've had anything like that. I mean, like I said before, I've been really stressed and drained by everything going on with the tracking. But I wouldn't say I've been depressed... not like, crying all day or feeling completely hopeless for weeks. Just really exhausted and on edge from having to deal with it all.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when the tracking and radio beliefs were still happening even though your mood was otherwise completely normal?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. I mean, like I said, I haven't really had any issues with my mood anyway, other than just being stressed out by the whole situation. The tracking and the comments on the radio... that just happens every day, regardless of how I'm feeling. So, yes, it definitely happens when I feel completely normal.\n\nDoctor: During this period, have you had a stretch of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: No, like I think I just mentioned a minute ago, I haven't had anything like that. I haven't been depressed for two weeks straight or anything. I'm just worn out from dealing with the tracking, but my mood itself is fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least a week when you felt unusually energized or needed much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I mean, I sleep okay most nights, considering everything. I've never had a time where I felt super energized or like I didn't need to sleep. Just my normal routine, really.\n\nDoctor: Have your work-related beliefs ever felt especially connected to feeling worthless, guilty, or like you’re being punished?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I don't feel guilty or worthless at all. And it doesn't really feel like a punishment... it's just what's happening. It's just the reality of them tracking me and the things they say. It doesn't have anything to do with me feeling bad about myself.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I've never heard voices or anything that wasn't actually there. It's just the stress and feeling stuck, like I mentioned. But no voices.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there’s any chance that the dispatch and radio comments might be a misunderstanding or explainable in some other way?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think it's a misunderstanding. People tell me it's just normal oversight, but I know what I'm seeing. Dispatch and compliance, they've been tracking my route changes through the computer system. And it's the little comments on the radio... I can tell certain drivers are reporting on me. It's not just a coincidence or a mistake. I know they're doing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any period during these two months when your mood was unusually depressed?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I've been depressed. I mean, I'm tired and stressed out from everything going on at work, and I just don't have the energy for things like choir or softball right now. But it's not like I'm sad or crying all the time. I just feel kind of blank and drained.\n\nDoctor: Have other people noticed that you seem emotionally flat or show much less expression than you used to?\n\nPatient: I don't know if they'd say I look flat, exactly, but... they've definitely noticed I'm not acting like myself. Like I mentioned, I just don't feel like singing with the choir or having the softball team over anymore. People have asked about that. And at home... most days after my shift, I just end up sitting at the kitchen table. I'll have my planner open, but I just can't make myself start dinner, or make any calls, or really do anything else. I just sort of sit there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a stretch of at least two weeks when you were still having the tracking or radio beliefs, but your mood was otherwise normal?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure how to answer that... I mean, since I started noticing the tracking and the comments, I haven't really felt normal at all. It's just been this same feeling the whole time. Just stressed and drained. \n\nThere hasn't really been a couple of weeks where I felt completely fine or like my old self while all that was going on. It's just been pretty much constant since it started.\n\nDoctor: Have these beliefs and the change in your functioning been going on continuously for at least six months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's been longer than six months. I'd say... this has really been building for about eight months now. It all started right around the time they put me in the temporary dispatch lead role at work.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen things that other people around you could not see?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I mean, I hear the comments, like I mentioned before, but... no, I don't see things. I've never seen anything that wasn't actually there.\n\nDoctor: During the past eight months, have you had any clear episode of major depression—meaning at least two weeks of feeling depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I've had anything like that. I mean, like I said before, I've been really stressed and drained by everything going on with the tracking. But I wouldn't say I've been depressed... not like, crying all day or feeling completely hopeless for weeks. Just really exhausted and on edge from having to deal with it all.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when the tracking and radio beliefs were still happening even though your mood was otherwise completely normal?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. I mean, like I said, I haven't really had any issues with my mood anyway, other than just being stressed out by the whole situation. The tracking and the comments on the radio... that just happens every day, regardless of how I'm feeling. So, yes, it definitely happens when I feel completely normal.\n\nDoctor: During this period, have you had a stretch of at least two weeks when you felt depressed most of the day nearly every day?\n\nPatient: No, like I think I just mentioned a minute ago, I haven't had anything like that. I haven't been depressed for two weeks straight or anything. I'm just worn out from dealing with the tracking, but my mood itself is fine.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least a week when you felt unusually energized or needed much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I mean, I sleep okay most nights, considering everything. I've never had a time where I felt super energized or like I didn't need to sleep. Just my normal routine, really.\n\nDoctor: Have your work-related beliefs ever felt especially connected to feeling worthless, guilty, or like you’re being punished?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I don't feel guilty or worthless at all. And it doesn't really feel like a punishment... it's just what's happening. It's just the reality of them tracking me and the things they say. It doesn't have anything to do with me feeling bad about myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever noticed any unusual bodily sensations or felt as though your thoughts were being put into your mind or taken out of it?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. My body feels normal. And my thoughts are definitely my own... I don't feel like anyone is putting them there or taking them away. It's really just the tracking and the things I hear them say, like I was talking about before. Nothing messing with my head or my body like that."
  }
